(version_key(p), str(p)))\n   150→    return matches[-1]\n   151→\n   152→\n   153→def load_trials_table(session_path: Path) -> pd.DataFrame:\n   154→    trial_file = pick_one_file(session_path / \"alf\", \"_ibl_trials.table.pqt\")\n   155→    if trial_file is None:\n   156→        raise FileNotFoundError(f\"Missing trial table for {session_path}\")\n   157→    return pd.read_parquet(trial_file)\n   158→\n   159→\n   160→def compute_trial_mask(trials: pd.DataFrame) -> np.ndarray:\n   161→    required = [\n   162→        \"stimOn_times\",\n   163→        \"choice\",\n   164→        \"feedback_times\",\n   165→        \"probabilityLeft\",\n   166→        \"firstMovement_times\",\n   167→        \"feedbackType\",\n   168→    ]\n   169→    mask = np.ones(len(trials), dtype=bool)\n   170→    rt = trials[\"firstMovement_times\"] - trials[\"stimOn_times\"]\n   171→    mask &= rt >= TRIAL_MASK_RT[0]\n   172→    mask &= rt <= TRIAL_MASK_RT[1]\n   173→    mask &= (trials[\"feedback_times\"] - trials[\"goCue_times\"]) <= MAX_TRIAL_LEN\n   174→    mask &= trials[\"choice\"] != 0\n   175→    for col in required:\n   176→        mask &= trials[col].notna().to_numpy()\n   177→    return mask\n   178→\n   179→\n   180→def compute_trial_number_in_block(prob_left: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   181→    counters = np.zeros(len(prob_left), dtype=np.float32)\n   182→    if len(prob_left) == 0:\n   183→        return counters\n   184→    count = 1\n   185→    counters[0] = count\n   186→    for i in range(1, len(prob_left)):\n   187→        if prob_left[i] == prob_left[i - 1]:\n   188→            count += 1\n   189→        else:\n   190→            count = 1\n   191→        counters[i] = count\n   192→    return counters\n   193→\n   194→\n   195→def load_probe_spikes_and_regions(\n   196→    probe_path: Path,\n   197→    br: BrainRegions,\n   198→    label_threshold: int = GOOD_CLUSTER_LABEL,\n   199→) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray, int, int]:\n   200→    pykilo_path = probe_path / \"pykilosort\"\n   201→    spikes_times_file = pick_one_file(pykilo_path, \"spikes.times.npy\")\n   202→    spikes_clusters_file = pick_one_file(pykilo_path, \"spikes.clusters.npy\")\n   203→    metrics_file = pick_one_file(pykilo_path, \"clusters.metrics.pqt\")\n   204→    clusters_channels_file = pick_one_file(pykilo_path, \"clusters.channels.npy\")\n   205→    channels_ids_file = pick_one_file(pykilo_path, \"channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017.npy\")\n   206→\n   207→    required = [\n   208→        spikes_times_file,\n   209→        spikes_clusters_file,\n   210→        metrics_file,\n   211→        clusters_channels_file,\n   212→        channels_ids_file,\n   213→    ]\n   214→    if any(x is None for x in required):\n   215→        raise FileNotFoundError(f\"Missing spike sorting assets under {probe_path}\")\n   216→\n   217→    metrics = pd.read_parquet(metrics_file, columns=[\"label\"])\n   218→    cluster_labels = metrics[\"label\"].to_numpy()\n   219→    good_mask = cluster_labels >= label_threshold\n   220→    selected_cluster_ids = np.flatnonzero(good_mask)\n   221→    if selected_cluster_ids.size == 0:\n   222→        return (\n   223→            np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64),\n   224→            np.empty(0, dtype=np.int32),\n   225→            np.empty(0, dtype=object),\n   226→            int(len(cluster_labels)),\n   227→            0,\n   228→        )\n   229→\n   230→    spikes_times = np.load(spikes_times_file, mmap_mode=\"r\")\n   231→    spikes_clusters = np.load(spikes_clusters_file, mmap_mode=\"r\")\n   232→    spike_keep = good_mask[spikes_clusters]\n   233→\n   234→    remap = np.full(cluster_labels.shape[0], -1, dtype=np.int32)\n   235→    remap[selected_cluster_ids] = np.arange(selected_cluster_ids.size, dtype=np.int32)\n   236→    selected_times = np.asarray(spikes_times[spike_keep], dtype=np.float64)\n   237→    selected_clusters = remap[np.asarray(spikes_clusters[spike_keep], dtype=np.int64)]\n   238→\n   239→    cluster_channels = np.asarray(np.load(clusters_channels_file), dtype=np.int64)\n   240→    channel_ids = np.asarray(np.load(channels_ids_file), dtype=np.int64)\n   241→    cluster_channels = cluster_channels[selected_cluster_ids]\n   242→    valid_channel = (cluster_channels >= 0) & (cluster_channels < len(channel_ids))\n   243→    cluster_region_ids = np.zeros(selected_cluster_ids.size, dtype=np.int64)\n   244→    cluster_region_ids[valid_channel] = channel_ids[cluster_channels[valid_channel]]\n   245→    cluster_regions = br.id2acronym(cluster_region_ids)\n   246→\n   247→    return (\n   248→        selected_times,\n   249→        selected_clusters,\n   250→        np.asarray(cluster_regions, dtype=object),\n   251→        int(len(cluster_labels)),\n   252→        int(selected_cluster_ids.size),\n   253→    )\n   254→\n   255→\n   256→def load_session_spikes(\n   257→    session_path: Path,\n   258→    br: BrainRegions,\n   259→    label_threshold: int = GOOD_CLUSTER_LABEL,\n   260→) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray, int, int]:\n   261→    probe_paths = sorted(\n   262→        [\n   263→            p\n   264→            for p in (session_path / \"alf\").glob(\"probe*\")\n   265→            if p.is_dir() and p.name.startswith(\"probe\")\n   266→        ]\n   267→    )\n   268→    merged_times = []\n   269→    merged_clusters = []\n   270→    merged_regions = []\n   271→    cluster_offset = 0\n   272→    total_clusters = 0\n   273→    good_clusters = 0\n   274→    for probe_path in probe_paths:\n   275→        times, clusters, regions, total_here, good_here = load_probe_spikes_and_regions(\n   276→            probe_path, br, label_threshold=label_threshold\n   277→        )\n   278→        total_clusters += total_here\n   279→        good_clusters += good_here\n   280→        if good_here == 0:\n   281→            continue\n   282→        merged_times.append(times)\n   283→        merged_clusters.append(clusters + cluster_offset)\n   284→        merged_regions.append(regions)\n   285→        cluster_offset += good_here\n   286→\n   287→    if cluster_offset == 0:\n   288→        return (\n   289→            np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64),\n   290→            np.empty(0, dtype=np.int32),\n   291→            np.empty(0, dtype=object),\n   292→            total_clusters,\n   293→            0,\n   294→        )\n   295→\n   296→    spike_times = np.concatenate(merged_times)\n   297→    spike_clusters = np.concatenate(merged_clusters)\n   298→    cluster_regions = np.concatenate(merged_regions)\n   299→    order = np.argsort(spike_times, kind=\"stable\")\n   300→    spike_times = spike_times[order]\n   301→    spike_clusters = spike_clusters[order]\n   302→    return spike_times, spike_clusters, cluster_regions, total_clusters, good_clusters\n   303→\n   304→\n   305→def bin_spikes_for_trials(\n   306→    spike_times: np.ndarray,\n   307→    spike_clusters: np.ndarray,\n   308→    n_clusters: int,\n   309→    align_times: np.ndarray,\n   310→    window: tuple[float, float] = TIME_WINDOW,\n   311→    binsize: float = BINSIZE_S,\n   312→) -> list[np.ndarray]:\n   313→    interval_len = window[1] - window[0]\n   314→    n_bins = int(np.ceil(interval_len / binsize))\n   315→    cluster_ids = np.arange(n_clusters, dtype=np.int32)\n   316→    intervals = np.c_[align_times + window[0], align_times + window[1]]\n   317→    results: list[np.ndarray] = []\n   318→    idx_starts = np.searchsorted(spike_times, intervals[:, 0], side=\"left\")\n   319→    idx_ends = np.searchsorted(spike_times, intervals[:, 1], side=\"left\")\n   320→    for idx0, idx1, (start, end) in zip(idx_starts, idx_ends, intervals):\n   321→        trial_counts = np.zeros((n_clusters, n_bins), dtype=np.float16)\n   322→        if idx1 > idx0:\n   323→            counts, _, cluster_idx = bincount2D(\n   324→                spike_times[idx0:idx1],\n   325→                spike_clusters[idx0:idx1],\n   326→                xbin=binsize,\n   327→                xlim=[start, end],\n   328→            )\n   329→            if counts.size:\n   330→                counts = counts[:, :n_bins]\n   331→                trial_counts[cluster_idx, : counts.shape[1]] = counts.astype(np.float16)\n   332→        results.append(trial_counts)\n   333→    return results\n   334→\n   335→\n   336→def load_wheel_speed(session_path: Path) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n   337→    wheel_pos_file = pick_one_file(session_path / \"alf\", \"_ibl_wheel.position.npy\")\n   338→    wheel_ts_file = pick_one_file(session_path / \"alf\", \"_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy\")\n   339→    if wheel_pos_file is None or wheel_ts_file is None:\n   340→        raise FileNotFoundError(f\"Missing wheel files for {session_path}\")\n   341→    pos = np.asarray(np.load(wheel_pos_file), dtype=np.float64)\n   342→    ts = np.asarray(np.load(wheel_ts_file), dtype=np.float64)\n   343→    if ts.ndim == 2 and ts.shape[1] == 2:\n   344→        ts = ts.mean(axis=1)\n   345→    pos_interp, ts_interp = interpolate_position(ts, pos, freq=1000)\n   346→    vel, _ = velocity_filtered(pos_interp, 1000)\n   347→    return ts_interp, np.abs(vel)\n   348→\n   349→\n   350→def load_whisker_motion_energy(session_path: Path) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, str]:\n   351→    for camera in (\"left\", \"right\"):\n   352→        me_file = pick_one_file(session_path / \"alf\", f\"{camera}Camera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy\")\n   353→        times_file = pick_one_file(session_path / \"alf\", f\"*{camera}Camera.times.npy\")\n   354→        if me_file is None or times_file is None:\n   355→            continue\n   356→        values = np.asarray(np.load(me_file), dtype=np.float64)\n   357→        times = np.asarray(np.load(times_file), dtype=np.float64)\n   358→        if len(values) != len(times):\n   359→            n = min(len(values), len(times))\n   360→            values = values[:n]\n   361→            times = times[:n]\n   362→        return times, values, camera\n   363→    raise FileNotFoundError(f\"Missing whisker motion energy files for {session_path}\")\n   364→\n   365→\n   366→def interpolate_behavior_trials(\n   367→    target_times: np.ndarray,\n   368→    target_values: np.ndarray,\n   369→    align_times: np.ndarray,\n   370→    window: tuple[float, float] = TIME_WINDOW,\n   371→    binsize: float = BINSIZE_S,\n   372→) -> tuple[list[np.ndarray | None], np.ndarray]:\n   373→    valid_source = np.isfinite(target_times) & np.isfinite(target_values)\n   374→    target_times = target_times[valid_source]\n   375→    target_values = target_values[valid_source]\n   376→    interval_len = window[1] - window[0]\n   377→    n_bins = int(np.ceil(interval_len / binsize))\n   378→    x_rel = np.linspace(window[0] + binsize, window[1], n_bins)\n   379→    outputs: list[np.ndarray | None] = []\n   380→    mask = np.zeros(len(align_times), dtype=bool)\n   381→\n   382→    idx_beg = np.searchsorted(target_times, align_times + window[0], side=\"right\")\n   383→    idx_end = np.searchsorted(target_times, align_times + window[1], side=\"left\")\n   384→    for i, align_time in enumerate(align_times):\n   385→        start = align_time + window[0]\n   386→        end = align_time + window[1]\n   387→        vals = target_values[idx_beg[i] : idx_end[i]]\n   388→        ts = target_times[idx_beg[i] : idx_end[i]]\n   389→        if len(vals) == 0:\n   390→            outputs.append(None)\n   391→            continue\n   392→        if np.abs(start - ts[0]) > binsize or np.abs(end - ts[-1]) > binsize:\n   393→            outputs.append(None)\n   394→            continue\n   395→        interp = interp1d(ts, vals, kind=\"linear\", fill_value=\"extrapolate\")\n   396→        outputs.append(interp(align_time + x_rel).astype(np.float32))\n   397→        mask[i] = True\n   398→    return outputs, mask\n   399→\n   400→\n   401→def map_choice_to_binary(choice_values: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   402→    mapped = np.full(choice_values.shape, -1, dtype=np.int16)\n   403→    mapped[choice_values == 1] = 0   # left\n   404→    mapped[choice_values == -1] = 1  # right\n   405→    if np.any(mapped < 0):\n   406→        raise ValueError(\"Unexpected choice values after masking\")\n   407→    return mapped\n   408→\n   409→\n   410→def map_prior_to_categorical(prob_left: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   411→    mapped = np.full(prob_left.shape, -1, dtype=np.int16)\n   412→    mapped[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.2)] = 0\n   413→    mapped[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.5)] = 1\n   414→    mapped[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.8)] = 2\n   415→    if np.any(mapped < 0):\n   416→        vals = np.unique(prob_left[mapped < 0])\n   417→        raise ValueError(f\"Unexpected probabilityLeft values: {vals}\")\n   418→    return mapped\n   419→\n   420→\n   421→def make_time_input() -> np.ndarray:\n   422→    return np.linspace(TIME_WINDOW[0] + BINSIZE_S, TIME_WINDOW[1], N_BINS, dtype=np.float32)\n   423→\n   424→\n   425→def compute_tertile_edges(values: Iterable[np.ndarray]) -> tuple[float, float]:\n   426→    flat_values = [np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel() for v in values if len(v)]\n   427→    if not flat_values:\n   428→        raise ValueError(\"Cannot compute tertile edges from empty values\")\n   429→    concat = np.concatenate(flat_values)\n   430→    q1, q2 = np.quantile(concat, [1 / 3, 2 / 3])\n   431→    if not np.isfinite(q1) or not np.isfinite(q2) or q1 >= q2:\n   432→        mn = float(np.min(concat))\n   433→        mx = float(np.max(concat))\n   434→        if mn == mx:\n   435→            eps = np.finfo(np.float32).eps\n   436→            return mn + eps, mn + 2 * eps\n   437→        step = (mx - mn) / 3.0\n   438→        return mn + step, mn + 2.0 * step\n   439→    return float(q1), float(q2)\n   440→\n   441→\n   442→def discretize_three_bins(values: np.ndarray, edges: tuple[float, float]) -> np.ndarray:\n   443→    return np.digitize(values, bins=np.array(edges, dtype=np.float32), right=False).astype(np.int16)\n   444→\n   445→\n   446→def format_seconds(seconds: float) -> str:\n   447→    if seconds < 60:\n   448→        return f\"{seconds:.1f}s\"\n   449→    minutes, sec = divmod(seconds, 60)\n   450→    return f\"{int(minutes)}m {sec:.1f}s\"\n   451→\n   452→\n   453→def process_session(spec: SessionSpec, br: BrainRegions) -> ProcessedSession | None:\n   454→    t0 = time.perf_counter()\n   455→    trials = load_trials_table(spec.session_path)\n   456→    raw_n_trials = len(trials)\n   457→    trial_mask = compute_trial_mask(trials)\n   458→    block_trial_number = compute_trial_number_in_block(trials[\"probabilityLeft\"].to_numpy())\n   459→\n   460→    spike_times, spike_clusters, cluster_regions, n_clusters_total, n_clusters_good = load_session_spikes(\n   461→        spec.session_path, br\n   462→    )\n   463→    if n_clusters_good == 0:\n   464→        print(f\"[skip] {spec.eid}: no good clusters after QC\")\n   465→        return None\n   466→\n   467→    try:\n   468→        wheel_times, wheel_speed = load_wheel_speed(spec.session_path)\n   469→        whisk_times, whisk_values, whisk_source = load_whisker_motion_energy(spec.session_path)\n   470→    except FileNotFoundError as exc:\n   471→        print(f\"[skip] {spec.eid}: {exc}\")\n   472→        return None\n   473→\n   474→    masked_trials = trials.loc[trial_mask].reset_index(drop=False)\n   475→    if len(masked_trials) < 2:\n   476→        print(f\"[skip] {spec.eid}: fewer than 2 trials after trial mask\")\n   477→        return None\n   478→\n   479→    align_times = masked_trials[\"stimOn_times\"].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)\n   480→    neural_trials = bin_spikes_for_trials(\n   481→        spike_times,\n   482→        spike_clusters,\n   483→        n_clusters=n_clusters_good,\n   484→        align_times=align_times,\n   485→    )\n   486→    wheel_trials, wheel_mask = interpolate_behavior_trials(wheel_times, wheel_speed, align_times)\n   487→    whisk_trials, whisk_mask = interpolate_behavior_trials(whisk_times, whisk_values, align_times)\n   488→    neural_mask = np.array([np.any(trial) for trial in neural_trials], dtype=bool)\n   489→    combined_mask = wheel_mask & whisk_mask & neural_mask\n   490→\n   491→    if combined_mask.sum() < 2:\n   492→        print(f\"[skip] {spec.eid}: fewer than 2 trials after behavior/neural alignment\")\n   493→        return None\n   494→\n   495→    neural_keep = [neural_trials[i] for i, keep in enumerate(combined_mask) if keep]\n   496→    wheel_keep = [wheel_trials[i] for i, keep in enumerate(combined_mask) if keep]\n   497→    whisk_keep = [whisk_trials[i] for i, keep in enumerate(combined_mask) if keep]\n   498→    masked_keep = masked_trials.loc[combined_mask].reset_index(drop=True)\n   499→\n   500→    time_input = make_time_input()\n   501→    inputs = []\n   502→    choice = []\n   503→    prior = []\n   504→    choice_vals = map_choice_to_binary(masked_keep[\"choice\"].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64))\n   505→    prior_vals = map_prior_to_categorical(masked_keep[\"probabilityLeft\"].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64))\n   506→    block_vals = block_trial_number[masked_keep[\"index\"].to_numpy()]\n   507→\n   508→    for block_num, choice_val, prior_val in zip(block_vals, choice_vals, prior_vals, strict=True):\n   509→        input_trial = np.vstack(\n   510→            [\n   511→                time_input,\n   512→                np.full(N_BINS, block_num, dtype=np.float32),\n   513→            ]\n   514→        ).astype(np.float32)\n   515→        inputs.append(input_trial)\n   516→        choice.append(np.full(N_BINS, choice_val, dtype=np.int16))\n   517→        prior.append(np.full(N_BINS, prior_val, dtype=np.int16))\n   518→\n   519→    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - t0\n   520→    print(\n   521→        f\"[ok] {spec.eid}: raw_trials={raw_n_trials}, kept_trials={len(neural_keep)}, \"\n   522→        f\"good_clusters={n_clusters_good}, whisker={whisk_source}, elapsed={format_seconds(elapsed)}\"\n   523→    )\n   524→    return ProcessedSession(\n   525→        eid=spec.eid,\n   526→        subject=spec.subject,\n   527→        neural=neural_keep,\n   528→        inputs=inputs,\n   529→        choice=choice,\n   530→        prior=prior,\n   531→        wheel_cont=[np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float32) for x in wheel_keep],\n   532→        whisker_cont=[np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float32) for x in whisk_keep],\n   533→        cluster_regions=np.asarray(cluster_regions, dtype=object),\n   534→        kept_trial_indices=masked_keep[\"index\"].to_numpy(dtype=np.int32),\n   535→        whisker_source=whisk_source,\n   536→        n_clusters_total=n_clusters_total,\n   537→        n_clusters_good=n_clusters_good,\n   538→        raw_n_trials=raw_n_trials,\n   539→        kept_n_trials=len(neural_keep),\n   540→    )\n   541→\n   542→\n   543→def process_session_worker(spec: SessionSpec) -> ProcessedSession | None:\n   544→    return process_session(spec, BrainRegions())\n   545→\n   546→\n   547→def plot_processing_summary(\n   548→    session: ProcessedSession,\n   549→    wheel_edges: tuple[float, float],\n   550→    whisker_edges: tuple[float, float],\n   551→    outdir: Path,\n   552→) -> None:\n   553→    if session.kept_n_trials == 0:\n   554→        return\n   555→    trial_idx = 0\n   556→    neural = session.neural[trial_idx].astype(np.float32)\n   557→    input_trial = session.inputs[trial_idx]\n   558→    wheel_cont = session.wheel_cont[trial_idx]\n ax[0].set_title(\"Binned Spike Counts\")\n   571→    ax[0].set_ylabel(\"Neuron\")\n   572→    fig.colorbar(im, ax=ax[0], fraction=0.046)\n   573→\n   574→    ax[1].plot(t, input_trial[0], label=\"time ax[2].set_title(\"Wheel Speed Continuous\")\n   585→    ax[2].set_ylabel(\"speed\")\n   586→\n   587→    ax[3].step(t, wheel_disc, where=\"mid\", color=\"tab:blue\")\n   588→    ax[3].axvline(0.0, align_time + window[0]\n        end = align_time + window[1]\n        vals = target_values[idx_beg[i] : idx_end[i]]\n        ts = target_times[idx_beg[i] : idx_end[i]]\n        if len(vals) == 0:\n            outputs.append(None)\n            continue\n        if np.abs(start - ts[0]) > binsize or np.abs(end - ts[-1]) > binsize:\n            outputs.append(None)\n            continue\n        interp = interp1d(ts, vals, kind=\"linear\", fill_value=\"extrapolate\")\n        outputs.append(interp(align_time + x_rel).astype(np.float32))\n        mask[i] = True\n    return outputs, mask\n\n\ndef map_choice_to_binary(choice_values: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    mapped = np.full(choice_values.shape, -1, dtype=np.int16)\n    mapped[choice_values == 1] = 0   # left\n    mapped[choice_values == -1] = 1  # right\n    if np.any(mapped < 0):\n        raise ValueError(\"Unexpected choice values after masking\")\n    return mapped\n\n\ndef map_prior_to_categorical(prob_left: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    mapped = np.full(prob_left.shape, -1, dtype=np.int16)\n    mapped[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.2)] = 0\n    mapped[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.5)] = 1\n    mapped[np.isclose(prob_left, 0.8)] = 2\n    if np.any(mapped < 0):\n        vals = np.unique(prob_left[mapped < 0])\n        raise ValueError(f\"Unexpected probabilityLeft values: {vals}\")\n    return mapped\n\n\ndef make_time_input() -> np.ndarray:\n    return np.linspace(TIME_WINDOW[0] + BINSIZE_S, TIME_WINDOW[1], N_BINS, dtype=np.float32)\n\n\ndef compute_tertile_edges(values: Iterable[np.ndarray]) -> tuple[float, float]:\n    flat_values = [np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel() for v in values if len(v)]\n    if not flat_values:\n        raise ValueError(\"Cannot compute tertile edges from empty values\")\n    concat = np.concatenate(flat_values)\n    q1, q2 = np.quantile(concat, [1 / 3, 2 / 3])\n    if not np.isfinite(q1) or not np.isfinite(q2) or q1 >= q2:\n        mn = float(np.min(concat))\n        mx = float(np.max(concat))\n        if mn == mx:\n            eps = np.finfo(np.float32).eps\n            return mn + eps, mn + 2 * eps\n        step = (mx - mn) / 3.0\n        return mn + step, mn + 2.0 * step\n    return float(q1), float(q2)\n\n\ndef discretize_three_bins(values: np.ndarray, edges: tuple[float, float]) -> prior.append(np.full(N_BINS, prior_val, dtype=np.int16))\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - t0\n    print(\n        f\"[ok] {spec.eid}: raw_trials={raw_n_trials}, kept_trials={len(neural_keep)}, Reading\n   121→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   122→\n   123→### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n   124→| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n   125→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   126→| Neurons (total) | 621,733 units in released dataset | “After applying these criteria, a total of 459 sessions, 699 insertions and 621,733 neurons remained” |\n   127→| Neurons / session | ~1,355 units/session in released dataset (621,733 / 459) | Derived from the released-dataset counts above |\n   128→| Subjects | 139 mice | “We trained 139 mice (94 male and 45 female)” |\n   129→| Sessions / subject | ~3.30 released sessions per mouse (459 / 139) | Derived from the released-dataset counts above |\n   130→| Trials (total) | Not directly stated as a total in searched text | Sessions “with at least 400 trials were retained” and later “459 sessions” are reported, but no paper-wide total trial count was stated in the extracted text |\n   131→| Trials / session | At least 400 retained for main analyses; at least 250 for release inclusion | “Only sessions with at least 400 trials were retained for further analyses”; “Sessions were included in the data release if the mice performed at least 250 trials” |\n   132→| Neural data time bin | 20 ms in Zhang code / generic model description; 50 ms stated for choice and prior in method-paper data-processing text | “Recordings are split into 2-s trials, each divided into 20-ms bins”; also “For choice… Within each trial, we segment neural activity into 50-ms non-overlapping time bins” |\n   133→| Behavior data time bin | Wheel / whisker sampled at camera or sensor resolution, then decoded in 20 ms bins for dynamic analyses | “wheel speed and whisker motion energy… are time-varying signals sampled at 60 Hz”; “Wheel values… averaged… in nonoverlapping 20-ms bins” |\n   134→| Reward rate / feedback availability | Not directly reported; release required at least 3 incorrect trials after exclusions | “sessions were included… if there were at least 3 trials with incorrect choices” |\n   135→| Unbiased block length | 90 trials | “For the first 90 trials, the stimulus appears randomly on either side with equal probability” |\n   136→| Biased block lengths | 20–100 trials, empirical mean 51 | “Blocks lasted for between 20 and 100 trials… (empirical mean of 51 trials)” |\n   137→| Reaction-time filter | 0.08–2.00 s from stim onset to first movement | “Trials were further excluded if the time between stimulus onset and the first movement of the wheel… were outside the range of 0.08–2.00 s” |\n   138→| Probe insertions | 699 insertions | “699 Neuropixels probes” / “699 insertions” |\n   139→| Well-isolated neurons | 75,708 | “Out of the 621,733 units collected, 75,708 were considered well-isolated neurons” |\n   140→| Canonical region set for data paper analyses | 201 regions, 62,857 neurons | “201 regions… for a total of 62,857 neurons” |\n   141→| Method-paper decoding set | 433 IBL sessions, 270 brain regions | “We apply our models to 433 IBL sessions… covering 270 brain regions” |\n   142→\n   143→\n   144→### Processing Details\n   145→- Task structure from the data paper:\n   146→  - 90 unbiased trials at `probabilityLeft = 0.5`\n   147→  - subsequent biased blocks alternate 0.2 / 0.8 left probability\n   148→  - block lengths drawn from a truncated distribution between 20 and 100 trials\n   149→- Data paper release / analysis curation:\n   150→  - released sessions required at least 250 trials, good high-contrast performance, at least 3 incorrect trials, and hardware QC pass\n   151→  - analyses in the paper retained sessions with at least 400 trials\n   152→  - trials excluded if any of `choice`, `probabilityLeft`, `feedbackType`, `feedback_times`, `stimOn_times`, `firstMovement_times` were missing\n   153→  - trials further excluded if `firstMovement_times - stimOn_times` was outside `[0.08, 2.00]` s\n   154→  - well-isolated neurons required amplitude > 50 µV, noise cutoff < 20 µV, and refractory-period criterion\n   155→- Method paper / decoder processing:\n   156→  - all modeled trials are 2 s windows\n   157→  - generic model description states 20 ms bins and `T = 100`\n   158→  - choice uses stimulus-onset alignment with window `[-0.5, 1.5]` s\n   159→  - prior uses stimulus-onset alignment with window `[-0.6, -0.1]` s in the text excerpt from `methods.txt`\n   160→  - dynamic behaviors (wheel speed, whisker motion energy) use first-movement alignment, from movement onset to +1.0 s, with 20 ms bins\n   161→  - the data paper’s own decoding section also describes wheel decoding as using bins from 200 ms before first movement to 1,000 ms after first movement, with a causal window of `W = 10` bins\n   162→- Behavioral stream definitions:\n   163→  - whisker motion energy is “the mean across pixels of the absolute value of the difference between adjacent frames” in a whisker-pad box\n   164→  - wheel values include speed and velocity from the rotary encoder\n   165→  - choice is binary\n   166→  - prior / block is a three-state latent or discrete task variable derived from block probabilities\n   167→\n   168→### Curation Steps\n   169→\n   170→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   171→- Data paper main analyses exclude units failing single-unit QC metrics: amplitude > 50 µV, noise cutoff < 20 µV, and refractory-period-violation criterion.\n   172→- Final region-level analyses further require at least 5 well-isolated neurons per session and at least two sessions for a region.\n   173→- The Zhang 2025 code path itself loads all spike-sorted clusters, then stores a `good_clusters` label in metadata; explicit neuron filtering is not applied in `prepare_data()`.\n   174→\n   175→**Trial curation rules**:\n   176→- Missing-event trial exclusion: `choice`, `probabilityLeft`, `feedbackType`, `feedback_times`, `stimOn_times`, `firstMovement_times`\n   177→- Reaction-time exclusion: keep only 0.08–2.00 s from `stimOn_times` to `firstMovement_times`\n   178→- Data-paper release inclusion also requires session-level quality and performance thresholds\n   179→- Zhang 2025 code additionally enforces `choice != 0` and `max_trial_len = 10.0` s via `load_trials_and_mask()`\n   180→\n   181→### Decoders Trained\n   182→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   183→| Choice | Method paper reports higher AUC than baseline; figure text exposes example values around `0.72`, `0.66`, `0.79` for compared models in Figure 4 / 5 context, but ordering is not completely recoverable from extracted text alone |\n   184→| Prior | Method paper reports higher Pearson correlation than baseline; extracted figure text shows example values around `0.65`, `0.05`, `0.34` for compared models |\n   185→| Wheel speed | Dynamic-behavior performance reported with `R2`; extracted Figure 8 text shows example IBL unaligned values up to roughly `0.55` and other examples up to `0.75` depending on task / model |\n   186→| Whisker motion energy | Dynamic-behavior performance reported with `R2`; extracted Figure 8 text shows example IBL unaligned values up to roughly `0.80` |\n   187→\n   188→---\n   189→\n   190→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   191→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   192→\n   193→### Discrepancies Found\n   194→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n   195→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   196→| Session universe | `bwm_release.csv` contains 459 release sessions / 699 probe insertions; method repo says decoders use 433 IBL sessions | Local cache has 461 concrete session directories, 441 with wheel + spikes + either whisker, 434 with left whisker specifically | Data paper release: 459 sessions; method paper: 433 sessions | Treat 459 sessions in `bwm_release.csv` as the canonical release index. The method-paper 433-session set is a downstream usable subset after additional availability / preprocessing constraints; I will derive usable sessions from actual preprocessing rather than force 433 a priori. |\n   197→| Alignment / time window | `0_data_caching.py` uses `align_time='stimOn_times'`, `time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)`, `binsize=0.02` for all cached behaviors | Raw data support stim-on and first-movement events | Method paper prose says choice uses stim onset, prior uses a pre-stimulus window, and wheel / whisker use first-movement alignment | For this conversion, use the executable repository settings of 2 s windows, 20 ms bins, and stimulus-onset alignment, because the user explicitly requires “Temporally align based on stimulus onset” and the reference code implements this directly. |\n   198→| Neural bin size | Reference code bins all trial-aligned data at 20 ms | Raw data are continuous spike times | Method paper has internal inconsistency: generic model description says 20 ms, but data-processing prose states 50 ms bins for choice / prior | Use 20 ms bins. This matches the executable code and the top-level model description (`T = 100` over 2 s). |\n   199→| Neuron filtering | `prepare_data()` loads all spike-sorted clusters; metadata stores `good_clusters = label >= 1` | Raw cluster metrics contain QC fields and labels; the full local release has 75,708 clusters with `label >= 1` | Data paper main analyses restrict to well-isolated neurons; method paper text says “all neurons, sorted by Kilosort 2.5” | For this export, keep `clusters.metrics.label >= 1`. This matches the data paper’s well-isolated-neuron count exactly in the 459-session local release and keeps the dense pickle tractable. |\n   200→| Trial filtering | `load_trials_and_mask()` excludes missing key events, RT outside `[0.08, 2.0]`, `choice == 0`, and `prepare_data()` also sets `max_trial_len=10.0` | Raw trial tables contain the needed event columns | Data paper excludes missing events and RT outside `[0.08, 2.0]`; no-choice exclusion is not emphasized there | Use the full reference-code trial mask, since it contains the paper overlap plus extra curation actually used by the decoder repository. |\n   201→| Whisker stream source | `bin_behaviors()` loads left whisker motion energy first, falls back to right if left is unavailable | Local cache has 434 left, 421 right, 441 either | Papers describe whisker motion energy generally, without fixing left vs right in the decoder section | Use left whisker motion energy when present, otherwise right, matching the reference code exactly. |\n   202→| Choice coding | Code reads `trials.choice` directly | Raw data encode choices as `-1`, `+1`, and `0` for no-go | Decoder task requires left = 0, right = 1 | After applying the no-choice mask, map the two remaining raw values to `{0, 1}` in the target export. |\n   203→| Prior / block coding | Code uses `trials.probabilityLeft` as `block` | Raw values are `0.2`, `0.5`, `0.8` | Papers describe unbiased 0.5 block then biased 0.2/0.8 blocks | Export prior as the user-required categorical mapping `0.2 -> 0`, `0.5 -> 1`, `0.8 -> 2`. |\n   204→| Dynamic-behavior decoding target construction | Zhang code caches wheel / whisker as interpolated time-varying traces on the common 20 ms trial grid | Raw wheel / motion-energy arrays are continuous streams with their own sampling | Data paper’s own wheel decoder description uses a causal window around movement and regression `R2` | For this project, dynamic outputs will be discretized after interpolation onto the shared stimulus-onset grid, because the target format requires categorical outputs and the user requested a single common alignment event. |\n   205→\n   206→### Final Understanding\n   207→- The most defensible reference for the conversion mechanics is the Zhang 2025 repository’s preprocessing code, not every detail of the prose papers, because the papers and code are not fully internally consistent.\n   208→- The data paper supplies the global dataset facts and core curation rules: 139 mice, 459 release sessions, 699 insertions, 621,733 units, RT and missing-event exclusions, and the definition of well-isolated neurons.\n   209→- The Zhang repository supplies the concrete session-processing pipeline used for decoder-ready data:\n   210→  - merge probes within session\n   211→  - load spike-sorted clusters and expose QC metadata\n   212→  - mask trials with missing critical events / out-of-range RT / no-choice / long trial\n   213→  - interpolate behavior streams onto a fixed 20 ms grid\n   214→  - create 2 s trial windows aligned to `stimOn_times`\n   215→- The user’s decoder task explicitly overrides any remaining ambiguity by requiring stimulus-onset alignment and categorical outputs for all decoded variables.\n   216→- Therefore the conversion plan for later steps will use:\n   217→  - session-level units of analysis\n   218→  - merged probes per session\n   219→  - `clusters.metrics.label >= 1` as the neuron inclusion rule\n   220→  - 20 ms bins over `[-0.5, 1.5]` s relative to stimulus onset\n   221→  - trial masking based on the Zhang code\n   222→  - left-then-right whisker fallback\n   223→  - categorical remapping for choice / prior and discretization for wheel speed / whisker motion energy\n   224→\n   225→---\n   226→\n   227→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   228→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   229→\n   230→### Variable Mapping\n   231→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   232→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   233→| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters` from all probes in one session | `neural` | Merge probes, keep clusters with `clusters.metrics.label >= 1`, bin counts into 20 ms bins over `[-0.5, 1.5]` s relative to `stimOn_times`; store each trial as `(n_neurons, 100)` float array | `load_spiking_data`, `merge_probes`, `bin_spiking_data` | Neural values will be raw binned spike counts, not standardized z-scores |\n   234→| common trial time grid | `input[0]` | Signed time since stimulus onset at each bin, repeated for every trial as a length-100 vector | Code uses `binsize=0.02`, `time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)`; behavior interpolation uses right-edge sample times | Planned values: `[-0.48, -0.46, ..., 1.50]` s to match behavior interpolation grid |\n   235→| raw `probabilityLeft` block sequence | `input[1]` | Trial number within current probability block, computed on the original trial sequence, then repeated across all 100 bins in the kept trial | Derived from trial table; no direct helper in repo | Block counter resets whenever `probabilityLeft` changes |\n   236→| raw `choice` | `output[0]` | Map raw IBL code to categorical side: `choice == 1 -> left -> 0`, `choice == -1 -> right -> 1`; repeat across all 100 bins | `bin_behaviors` loads `choice`; IBL docs / local examples define sign convention | Trials with `choice == 0` are excluded by reference mask |\n   237→| raw `probabilityLeft` | `output[1]` | Map `{0.2, 0.5, 0.8}` to `{0, 1, 2}` and repeat across all 100 bins | `bin_behaviors` loads this as `block` | User requested this categorical coding explicitly |\n   238→| wheel stream | `output[2]` | Load wheel velocity, take absolute value to obtain speed, interpolate onto stimulus-onset grid, then discretize into 3 global bins | `load_target_behavior('wheel-speed')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | Bins will be dataset-wide tertiles over valid aligned samples |\n   239→| whisker motion energy stream | `output[3]` | Load left whisker motion energy; if unavailable, fall back to right; interpolate onto stimulus-onset grid; discretize into 3 global bins | `load_target_behavior('left-whisker-motion-energy' / 'right-whisker-motion-energy')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | Bins will be dataset-wide tertiles over valid aligned samples |\n   240→| session subject metadata | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Build unique subject list from kept sessions and index each session into it | `bwm_release.csv` / session path metadata | Deterministic session order required |\n   241→| cluster acronyms | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Build global vocabulary of recorded acronyms and index each neuron | `clusters['acronym']` via `prepare_data` metadata | Use raw acronyms rather than Beryl remapping, because session export is neuron-level |\n   242→| session / conversion settings | `metadata` | Store task description, 20 ms bin size, `stimOn_times` alignment, offsets, discretization edges, release/source notes | Project-defined | Include enough fields to reproduce the export |\n   243→\n   244→### Key Decisions\n   245→1. **Use 2D time-varying arrays for both `input` and `output`**: Static trial variables (`choice`, `prior`, `trial_number_in_block`) will be repeated across the 100-bin time axis so every trial has consistent `(d, T)` tensors.\n   246→2. **Use the Zhang repository’s 2 s, 20 ms, stimulus-onset-aligned grid**: This matches the executable code and the user’s explicit alignment instruction, despite prose discrepancies in the papers.\n   247→3. **Keep clusters with `label >= 1`**: This matches the data paper’s well-isolated-unit count exactly in the full local release (`75,708`) and keeps the dense export tractable while remaining anchored to an explicit QC field already present in the reference code.\n   248→4. **Apply the reference trial mask before export**: Required events present, RT in `[0.08, 2.0]` s, `choice != 0`, and trial duration ≤ 10 s.\n   249→5. **Require sessions to have all core streams needed by this task**: spikes, trials, wheel, and at least one whisker-motion-energy camera; afterwards, require at least 2 valid kept trials per session.\n   250→6. **Use left whisker motion energy first, then right as fallback**: This exactly matches `bin_behaviors()` and maximizes session retention without inventing a new rule.\n   251→7. **Discretize wheel speed and whisker motion energy using global tertiles**: Global edges preserve a common categorical meaning across sessions and should keep class balance better than fixed-width bins.\n   252→8. **Compute trial number in block on the original unfiltered trial table**: Excluded trials should not renumber the latent block progression.\n   253→9. **Map choice polarity using rewarded nonzero-contrast trials**: Local data confirm `choice == 1` corresponds to left and `choice == -1` to right, so export will use left=0, right=1.\n   254→10. **Preserve raw region acronyms in `brain_regions`**: This avoids collapsing neurons across atlas levels and keeps the export closest to the cluster metadata.\n   255→\n   256→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   257→- [ ] Neural sanity check: for a chosen session/trial/neuron/bin, compare exported spike count to a direct count from raw `spikes.times` / `spikes.clusters` within the corresponding raw bin boundaries using `np.allclose()`.\n   258→- [ ] Input sanity check: verify exported `time_since_stimulus_onset` exactly matches the constructed common grid and exported `trial_number_in_block` matches a direct raw-table block counter for selected trials.\n   259→- [ ] Output sanity check 1: verify exported choice / prior values for selected trials against raw `trials.choice` and `trials.probabilityLeft` using direct remapping.\n   260→- [ ] Output sanity check 2: verify aligned continuous wheel speed / whisker values at selected trial-bin locations against direct interpolation from raw streams before discretization, then verify discretized class assignments from stored global bin edges.\n   261→- [ ] Session-count sanity check: compare numbers of kept sessions, trials, and neurons against the release table, the local cache, and the paper-reported totals to explain every reduction.\n   262→\n   263→---\n   264→\n   265→## Step 6: Script optional QC thresholding exists but is not used by `prepare_data`. |\n| `merge_probes` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Merge probes within a session, reindex cluster ids, sort spikes by time. |\n| `load_trials_and_mask` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | CURATION | Load trials and create trial mask using reaction time / NaN / duration / no-choice filters. |\n| `load_target_behavior` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | LOADING | Load wheel, whisker motion energy, pupil, and DLC-derived behavior streams from IBL loaders. |\n| `get_behavior_per_interval` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Slice continuous behavior into trial-aligned windows and linearly interpolate to fixed bins. |\n| `bin_behaviors` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Build per-trial variables (`choice`, `block`, `reward`, `contrast`) and time-varying binned behavior streams. |\n| `bin_spiking_data` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Align spikes to trial events and bin into fixed-width time bins; returns trial arrays. |\n| `prepare_data` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | LOADING | End-to-end session assembly: probes, trials, anytime behaviors, metadata. |\n| `align_spike_behavior` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | CURATION | Remove trials with missing behavior / failed mask and ensure neural and behavior trial counts match. |\n| `create_dataset` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/dataset_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Store per-trial spike matrices plus behavior and metadata in HuggingFace dataset format. |\n| `SingleSessionDataset` | `code/code_zhang2025/src/utils/data_loader_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Reconstruct cached spike arrays, standardize spikes, and encode targets for decoding. |\n| `src/0_data_caching.py` pipeline | `code/code_zhang2025/src/0_data_caching.py` | PROCESSING | Reference orchestration script for session selection, preprocessing, alignment, and train/val/test partitioning. |\n\n### Notes\n- Reference code is for electrophysiology, not imaging. No delta-F/F computation is involved.\n- Reference preprocessing aligns trials to `stimOn_times` with `time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)` seconds and `binsize=0.02` seconds, giving 2 s trial windows in 20 ms bins.\n- `prepare_data()` merges all probes from an `eid` into one session representation before decoding.\n- Trial curation in `load_trials_and_mask()` uses defaults `min_rt=0.08`, `max_rt=2.0`, excludes NaNs in `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`, excludes `choice == 0`, and in `prepare_data()` additionally enforces `max_trial_len=10.0`.\n- The Zhang 2025 caching script calls `load_spiking_data()` without `qc`, so it loads all labeled clusters, but metadata preserves a `good_clusters` flag computed as `clusters['label'] >= 1`.\n- Brain regions are taken from cluster acronyms and can be remapped to Beryl for region-level decoding; the caching script uses all regions together by default (`single_region=False`, `region='all'`).\n- For time-varying behavior, `get_behavior_per_interval()` interpolates each stream onto the aligned bin grid and rejects intervals that start too late, end too early, or contain disallowed NaNs.\n- `bin_behaviors()` constructs per-trial variables directly from the trials table:\n  - `choice` from `trials.choice`\n  - `block` from `trials.probabilityLeft`\n  - `reward` from `rewardVolume > 1`\n  - `contrast` from signed contrast difference\n- `align_spike_behavior()` is intended to drop invalid trials, but the boolean-mask code is weak (`target_mask and beh_mask` on Python lists). I need to treat the function as conceptual guidance and verify actual intended masking against data/text in later steps.\n- Downstream decoding treats `choice` as classification and wheel / whisker / pupil as regression in the Zhang code. Our target format differs because the user requires categorical outputs for all decoder outputs.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- `data/` trials were retained for further analyses”; “Sessions were included in the data release if the mice performed at least 250 trials” |\n| Neural data time bin | 20 ms in Zhang code / generic model segment neural activity into 50-ms non-overlapping time bins” |\n| Behavior data time bin | Wheel / whisker sampled at camera or sensor resolution, then decoded in 20 ms bins for dynamic analyses | “wheel speed and whisker motion energy… are time-varying signals sampled at 60 Hz”; “Wheel values… averaged… in nonoverlapping 20-ms bins” |\n| Reward rate / feedback availability | Not directly reported; release required at least 3 incorrect trials after exclusions | “sessions were included… if there were at least 3 trials with incorrect choices” |\n| Unbiased block length | 90 trials | “For the first 90 trials, the stimulus appears randomly on either side with equal probability” |\n| Biased block lengths | 20–100 trials, empirical mean 51 | “Blocks lasted for between 20 and 100 trials… (empirical mean of 51 trials)” |\n| Reaction-time filter | 0.08–2.00 s from stim onset to first movement | “Trials were further excluded if the time between stimulus onset and the first movement of the wheel… were outside the range of 0.08–2.00 s” |\n| Probe insertions | 699 insertions | “699 Neuropixels probes” / “699 insertions” |\n| Well-isolated neurons | 75,708 | “Out of the 621,733 units collected, 75,708 were considered well-isolated neurons” |\n| Canonical region set for data paper analyses | 201 regions, 62,857 neurons | “201 regions… for a total of 62,857 neurons” |\n| Method-paper decoding set | 433 IBL sessions, 270 brain regions | “We apply our models to 433 IBL sessions… covering 270 brain regions” |\n\n\n### Processing Details\n- Task structure from the data paper:\n  - 90 unbiased trials at `probabilityLeft = 0.5`\n  - subsequent biased blocks alternate 0.2 / 0.8 left probability\n  - block lengths drawn from a truncated distribution between 20 and 100 trials\n- Data paper release / analysis curation:\n  - released sessions required at least 250 trials, good high-contrast performance, at least 3 incorrect trials, and hardware QC pass\n  - analyses in the paper retained sessions with at least 400 trials\n  - trials excluded if any of `choice`, `probabilityLeft`, `feedbackType`, `feedback_times`, `stimOn_times`, `firstMovement_times` were missing\n  - trials further excluded if `firstMovement_times - stimOn_times` was outside `[0.08, 2.00]` s\n  - well-isolated neurons required amplitude > 50 µV, noise cutoff < 20 µV, and refractory-period criterion\n- Method paper / decoder processing:\n  - all modeled trials are 2 s windows\n  - generic model description states 20 ms bins and `T = 100`\n  - choice uses stimulus-onset alignment with window `[-0.5, 1.5]` s\n  - prior uses stimulus-onset alignment with window `[-0.6, -0.1]` s in the text excerpt from `methods.txt`\n  - dynamic behaviors (wheel speed, whisker motion energy) use first-movement alignment, from movement onset to +1.0 s, with 20 ms bins\n  - the data paper’s own decoding section also describes wheel decoding as using bins from 200 ms before first movement to 1,000 ms after first movement, with a causal window of `W = 10` bins\n- Behavioral stream definitions:\n  - whisker motion energy is “the mean across pixels of the absolute value of the difference between adjacent frames” in a whisker-pad box\n  - wheel values include speed and velocity from the rotary encoder\n  - choice is binary\n  - prior / block is a three-state latent or discrete task variable derived from block probabilities\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Data paper main analyses exclude units failing single-unit QC metrics: amplitude > 50 µV, noise cutoff < 20 µV, and refractory-period-violation criterion.\n- Final region-level analyses further require at least 5 well-isolated neurons per session and at least two sessions for a region.\n- The Zhang 2025 code path itself loads all spike-sorted clusters, then stores a `good_clusters` label in metadata; explicit neuron filtering is not applied in `prepare_data()`.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Missing-event trial exclusion: `choice`, `probabilityLeft`, `feedbackType`, `feedback_times`, `stimOn_times`, `firstMovement_times`\n- Reaction-time exclusion: keep only 0.08–2.00 s from `stimOn_times` to `firstMovement_times`\n- Data-paper release inclusion also requires session-level quality and performance thresholds\n- Zhang 2025 code additionally enforces `choice != 0` and `max_trial_len = 10.0` s via `load_trials_and_mask()`\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| Choice | Method paper reports higher AUC than baseline; figure text exposes example values around `0.72`, `0.66`, `0.79` for compared models in Figure 4 / 5 context, but ordering is not completely recoverable from extracted text alone |\n| Prior | Method paper reports higher Pearson correlation than baseline; extracted figure text shows example values around `0.65`, `0.05`, `0.34` for compared models |\n| Wheel speed | Dynamic-behavior performance reported with `R2`; extracted Figure 8 text shows example IBL unaligned values up to roughly `0.55` and other examples up to `0.75` depending on task / model |\n| Whisker motion energy | Dynamic-behavior performance reported with `R2`; extracted Figure 8 text shows example IBL unaligned values up to roughly `0.80` |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Session universe | `bwm_release.csv` contains 459 release sessions / 699 probe insertions; method repo says decoders use 433 IBL sessions | Local cache has 461 concrete session directories, 441 with wheel + spikes + either whisker, 434 with left whisker specifically | Data paper release: 459 sessions; method paper: 433 sessions | Treat 459 sessions in `bwm_release.csv` as the canonical release index. The method-paper 433-session set is a downstream usable subset after additional availability / preprocessing constraints; I will derive usable sessions from actual preprocessing rather than force 433 a priori. |\n| Alignment / time window | `0_data_caching.py` uses `align_time='stimOn_times'`, `time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)`, `binsize=0.02` for all cached behaviors | Raw data support stim-on and first-movement events | Method paper prose says choice uses stim onset, prior uses a pre-stimulus window, and wheel / whisker use first-movement alignment | For this conversion, use the executable repository settings of 2 s windows, 20 ms bins, and stimulus-onset alignment, because the user explicitly requires “Temporally align based on stimulus onset” and the reference code implements this directly. |\n| Neural bin size | Reference code bins all trial-aligned data at 20 ms | Raw data are continuous spike times | Method paper has internal inconsistency: generic model description says 20 ms, but data-processing prose states 50 ms bins for choice / prior | Use 20 ms bins. This matches the executable code and the top-level model description (`T = 100` over 2 s). |\n| Neuron filtering | `prepare_data()` loads all spike-sorted clusters; metadata stores `good_clusters = label >= 1` | Raw cluster metrics contain QC fields and labels; the full local release has 75,708 clusters with `label >= 1` | Data paper main analyses restrict to well-isolated neurons; method paper text says “all neurons, sorted by Kilosort 2.5” | For this export, keep `clusters.metrics.label >= 1`. This matches the data paper’s well-isolated-neuron count exactly in the 459-session local release and keeps the dense pickle tractable. |\n| Trial filtering | `load_trials_and_mask()` excludes missing key events, RT outside `[0.08, 2.0]`, `choice == 0`, and `prepare_data()` also sets `max_trial_len=10.0` | Raw trial tables contain the needed event columns | Data paper excludes missing events and RT outside `[0.08, 2.0]`; no-choice exclusion is not emphasized there | Use the full reference-code trial mask, since it contains the paper overlap plus extra curation actually used by the decoder repository. |\n| Whisker stream source | `bin_behaviors()` loads left whisker motion energy first, falls back to right if left is unavailable | Local cache has 434 left, 421 right, 441 either | Papers describe whisker motion energy generally, without fixing left vs right in the decoder section | Use left whisker motion energy when present, otherwise right, matching the reference code exactly. |\n| Choice coding | Code reads `trials.choice` directly | Raw data encode choices as `-1`, `+1`, and `0` for no-go | Decoder task requires left = 0, right = 1 | After applying the no-choice mask, map the two remaining raw values to `{0, 1}` in the target export. |\n| Prior / block coding | Code uses `trials.probabilityLeft` as `block` | Raw values are `0.2`, `0.5`, `0.8` | Papers describe unbiased 0.5 block then biased 0.2/0.8 blocks | Export prior as the user-required categorical mapping `0.2 -> 0`, `0.5 -> 1`, `0.8 -> 2`. |\n| Dynamic-behavior decoding target construction | Zhang code caches wheel / whisker as interpolated time-varying traces on the common 20 ms trial grid | Raw wheel / motion-energy arrays are continuous streams with their own sampling | Data paper’s own wheel decoder description uses a causal window around movement and regression `R2` | For this project, dynamic outputs will be discretized after interpolation onto the shared stimulus-onset grid, because the target format requires categorical outputs and the user requested a single common alignment event. |\n\n### Final Understanding\n- The most defensible reference for the conversion mechanics is the Zhang 2025 repository’s preprocessing code, not every detail of the prose papers, because the papers and code are not fully internally consistent.\n- The data paper supplies the global dataset facts and core curation rules: 139 mice, 459 release sessions, 699 insertions, 621,733 units, RT and missing-event exclusions, and the definition of well-isolated neurons.\n- The Zhang repository supplies the concrete session-processing pipeline used for decoder-ready data:\n  - merge probes within session\n  - load spike-sorted clusters and expose QC metadata\n  - mask trials with missing critical events / out-of-range RT / no-choice / long trial\n  - interpolate behavior streams onto a fixed 20 ms grid\n  - create 2 s trial windows aligned to `stimOn_times`\n- The user’s decoder task explicitly overrides any remaining ambiguity by requiring stimulus-onset alignment and categorical outputs for all decoded variables.\n- Therefore the conversion plan for later steps will use:\n  - session-level units of analysis\n  - merged probes per session\n  - `clusters.metrics.label >= 1` as the neuron inclusion rule\n  - 20 ms bins over `[-0.5, 1.5]` s relative to stimulus onset\n  - trial masking based on the Zhang code\n  - left-then-right whisker fallback\n  - categorical remapping for choice / prior and discretization for wheel speed / whisker motion energy\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters` from all probes in one session | `neural` | Merge probes, keep clusters with `clusters.metrics.label >= 1`, bin counts into 20 ms bins over `[-0.5, 1.5]` s relative to `stimOn_times`; store each trial as `(n_neurons, 100)` float array | `load_spiking_data`, `merge_probes`, `bin_spiking_data` | Neural values will be raw binned spike counts, not standardized z-scores |\n| common trial time grid | `input[0]` | Signed time since stimulus onset at each bin, repeated for every trial as a length-100 vector | Code uses `binsize=0.02`, `time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)`; behavior interpolation uses right-edge sample times | Planned values: `[-0.48, -0.46, ..., 1.50]` s to match behavior interpolation grid |\n| raw `probabilityLeft` block sequence | `input[1]` | Trial number within current probability block, computed on the original trial sequence, then repeated across all 100 bins in the kept trial | Derived from trial table; no direct helper in repo | Block counter resets whenever `probabilityLeft` changes |\n| raw `choice` | `output[0]` | Map raw IBL code to categorical side: `choice == 1 -> left -> 0`, `choice == -1 -> right -> 1`; repeat across all 100 bins | `bin_behaviors` loads `choice`; IBL docs / local examples define sign convention | Trials with `choice == 0` are excluded by reference mask |\n| raw `probabilityLeft` | `output[1]` | Map `{0.2, 0.5, 0.8}` to `{0, 1, 2}` and repeat across all 100 bins | `bin_behaviors` loads this as `block` | User requested this categorical coding explicitly |\n| wheel stream | `output[2]` | Load wheel velocity, take absolute value to obtain speed, interpolate onto stimulus-onset grid, then discretize into 3 global bins | `load_target_behavior('wheel-speed')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | Bins will be dataset-wide tertiles over valid aligned samples |\n| whisker motion energy stream | `output[3]` | Load left whisker motion energy; if unavailable, fall back to right; interpolate onto stimulus-onset grid; discretize into 3 global bins | `load_target_behavior('left-whisker-motion-energy' / 'right-whisker-motion-energy')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | Bins will be dataset-wide tertiles over valid aligned samples |\n| session subject metadata | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Build unique subject list from kept sessions and index each session into it | `bwm_release.csv` / session path metadata | Deterministic session order required |\n| cluster acronyms | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Build global vocabulary of recorded acronyms and index each neuron | `clusters['acronym']` via `prepare_data` metadata | Use raw acronyms rather than Beryl remapping, because session export is neuron-level |\n| session / conversion settings | `metadata` | Store task description, 20 ms bin size, `stimOn_times` alignment, offsets, discretization edges, release/source notes | Project-defined | Include enough fields to reproduce the export |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Use 2D time-varying arrays for both `input` and `output`**: Static trial variables (`choice`, `prior`, `trial_number_in_block`) will be repeated across the 100-bin time axis so every trial has consistent `(d, T)` tensors.\n2. **Use the Zhang repository’s 2 s, 20 ms, stimulus-onset-aligned grid**: This matches the executable code and the user’s explicit alignment instruction, despite prose discrepancies in the papers.\n3. **Keep clusters with `label >= 1`**: This matches the data paper’s well-isolated-unit count exactly in the full local release (`75,708`) and keeps the dense export tractable while remaining anchored to an explicit QC field already present in the reference code.\n4. **Apply the reference trial mask before export**: Required events present, RT in `[0.08, 2.0]` s, `choice != 0`, and trial duration ≤ 10 s.\n5. **Require sessions to have all core streams needed by this task**: spikes, trials, wheel, and at least one whisker-motion-energy camera; afterwards, require at least 2 valid kept trials per session.\n6. **Use left whisker motion energy first, then right as fallback**: This exactly matches `bin_behaviors()` and maximizes session retention without inventing a new rule.\n7. **Discretize wheel speed and whisker motion energy using global tertiles**: Global edges preserve a common categorical meaning across sessions and should keep class balance better than fixed-width bins.\n8. **Compute trial number in block on the original unfiltered trial table**: Excluded trials should not renumber the latent block progression.\n9. **Map choice polarity using rewarded nonzero-contrast trials**: Local data confirm `choice == 1` corresponds to left and `choice == -1` to right, so export will use left=0, right=1.\n10. **Preserve raw region acronyms in `brain_regions`**: This avoids collapsing neurons across atlas levels and keeps the export closest to the cluster metadata.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Neural sanity check: for a chosen session/trial/neuron/bin, compare exported spike count to a direct count from raw `spikes.times` / `spikes.clusters` within the corresponding raw bin boundaries using `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] Input sanity check: verify exported `time_since_stimulus_onset` exactly matches the constructed common grid and exported `trial_number_in_block` matches a direct raw-table block counter for selected trials.\n- [ ] Output sanity check 1: verify exported choice / prior values for selected trials against raw `trials.choice` and `trials.probabilityLeft` using direct remapping.\n- [ ] Output sanity check 2: verify aligned continuous wheel speed / whisker values at selected trial-bin locations against direct interpolation from raw streams before discretization, then verify discretized class assignments from stored global bin edges.\n- [ ] Session-count sanity check: compare numbers of kept sessions, trials, and neurons against the release table, the local cache, and the paper-reported totals to explain every reduction.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n- Implemented Example figure values are in the moderate-above-chance regime (`~0.66` to `~0.79` AUC depending on model/example), so our result is directionally consistent but not directly numerically comparable. |\n| prior_probability_of_left | Validation balanced accuracy = `0.6708` (`2.012x` chance) | Methods paper evaluates continuous prior with Pearson correlation rather than 3-class accuracy. Example figure values show clear improvement over baseline (example correlations from weak single-session values to substantially stronger multi-session/oracle values), so our strong 3-class accuracy is qualitatively consistent. |\n| wheel_speed_bin | Validation balanced accuracy = `0.6408` (`1.922x` chance) | Methods paper evaluates continuous wheel speed with `R²`, plus qualitative reconstructions. Direct numerical comparison is not possible because this task requires 3-bin discretization and stimulus-onset alignment instead of the paper’s movement-aligned continuous regression. |\n| whisker_motion_energy_bin | Validation balanced accuracy = `0.7409` (`2.223x` chance) | Methods paper evaluates continuous whisker motion energy with `R²`, plus qualitative reconstructions. Direct numerical comparison is not possible because this task requires 3-bin discretization and stimulus-onset alignment instead of the paper’s movement-aligned continuous regression. |\n\nChoice is the only output below the Step-12 heuristic threshold of `1.5x` chance. I investigated whether this indicates a conversion bug:\n\n- Raw-label spot checks from Step 10 already confirmed the exported choice labels match the raw `trials.choice` values on multiple trials via `np.allclose()`.\n- Alignment for choice is stimulus onset, matching both the Decoder Task and the methods text.\n- The full-run train/validation ratio for choice is only `1.038`, so there is no sign of severe overfitting or leakage.\n- The methods paper does not report near-ceiling choice decoding; instead it reports moderate, model-dependent improvement. Given the metric mismatch (paper uses AUC, current validation uses balanced accuracy) and the fact that our decoder task pools all exported sessions rather than reproducing the paper’s five-region benchmark, the current choice performance does not indicate a conversion error.\n\nFor the other three outputs, validation accuracy is comfortably above both chance and the `1.5x`-chance heuristic, and train/validation gaps are all small:\n\n- `prior_probability_of_left`: train/val ratio `1.031`\n- `wheel_speed_bin`: train/val ratio `1.011`\n- `whisker_motion_energy_bin`: train/val ratio `1.006`\n\nThis argues against leakage, severe overfitting, or a major temporal misalignment in the converted data.\n\n### Issues Found and Resolved\n- **GPU memory failure during full decoder training**: The default CUDA run failed with `torch.OutOfMemoryError`; reran Step 11 with `--cpu`, completed training, and recorded the final metrics from the successful CPU run.\n- **Potential low-choice-accuracy concern**: Reviewed raw labels, alignment choice, class balance, and train/validation gap. No conversion bug was found, so no code change was warranted.\n\n---\n\n## Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n- [x] README.md created\n- [x] cache/ folder created\n- [x] All files organized\n\nNotes:\n- `README.md` summarizes the converted dataset, exported variables, file structure, and reproduction commands.\n- `cache/README_CACHE.md` documents the cache folder and notes that no standalone investigation scripts were retained.\n- Required conversion outputs remain in the project root so they are easy to inspect and so the expected filenames still exist.\n","numLines":515,"startLine":1,"totalLines":515}}}

Some data may be missing and may need to be downloaded. \n    33→- **Documentation** about how to access the data can be found at https://docs.internationalbrainlab.org/public_docs/public_introduction.html.\n    34→\n    35→## Decoder Task\n    36→\n    37→Temporally align based on stimulus onset\n    38→\n    39→### Decoder Inputs:\n    40→- Time since stimulus onset, continuous, time-varying\n    41→- Trial number in block, continuous, per-trial\n    42→\n    43→### Decoder Outputs\n    44→- Choice, binary, per-trial, left = 0, right = 1\n    45→- Prior probability of left, per-trial, 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2\n    46→- Wheel speed discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n    47→- Whisker motion energy discretized into 3 bins, time-varying\n    48→\n    49→## Target Data Format\n    50→\n    51→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n    52→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    53→\n    54→```python\n    55→data = {\n    56→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    57→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    58→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    59→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    60→            ...\n    61→        ],\n    62→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    63→            ...\n    64→        ],\n    65→        ...\n    66→    ],\n    67→\n    68→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    69→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    70→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    71→            ...\n    72→        ],\n    73→        ...\n    74→    ],\n    75→\n    76→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    77→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    78→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    79→            ...\n    80→        ],\n    81→        ...\n    82→    ],\n    83→\n    84→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    85→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    86→\n    87→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    88→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n    89→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    90→    ] # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n    91→\n    92→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    93→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    94→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n    95→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n    96→        ...\n    97→    ],\n    98→\n    99→    'metadata': {\n   100→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n   101→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n   102→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n   103→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   104→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   105→        ...\n   106→    }\n   107→}\n   108→```\n   109→\n   110→### Key Format Requirements\n   111→\n   112→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   113→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   114→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   115→  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n   116→\n   117→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   118→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   119→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n   120→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   121→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   122→    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n   123→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   124→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   125→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   126→\n   127→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   128→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   129→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   130→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   131→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   132→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   133→\n   134→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   135→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   136→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   137→\n   138→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   139→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   140→\n   141→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   142→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   143→\n   144→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   145→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   146→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   147→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   148→\n   149→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   150→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   151→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   152→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   153→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   154→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   155→\n   156→## Python environment\n   157→\n   158→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   159→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   160→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   161→\n   162→## Decoder Reference\n   163→\n   164→- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   165→- Optional arguments:\n   166→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   167→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   168→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   169→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   170→\n   171→### Purpose\n   172→\n   173→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   174→1. If full conversion time estimate is longer than 15 minutes, speed up the code by writing more efficient code (vectorizing loops, removing unnecessary or redundant computations) and/or including parallel processing. \n   387→3.
